Proposition 6.8 (Boldness is a two-sided trade-off). [T2 Prop 2.9(d),(e), added after verification] (a) On
the class of Proposition 6.3(b) with uniform prior, every learner with R̂t ⊆ R for some R ∈ VSt
in every round, in particular every OH learner, makes 2k − 1 incompleteness errors on some text
                                                            S
for some target. The non-oligarchic union learner R̂t = VSt makes no error of either type
there, and is silently unsound. (b) On the class of Theorem 3.12, no accepted set guarantees that
every possible incompleteness error and every possible detection deletes half the weight.

    Proof in Appendix E.2. Reading. Oligarchy makes each contradiction worth a bit, robustly.
It does not make boldness cheap: on this class every oligarchic learner, including the boldest,
pays exactly the price of caution in missed valid steps, and a learner that avoids this price must
at some round accept a set inside no surviving hypothesis, hence a target-invalid step. The
two-sided minimax is open (Section 14); Section 7 resolves the trade-off architecturally, with
cautious assertion and a bold sandbox under attack.

What coherence cannot see. Now let hypotheses be finitary consequence relations with
target ⊢∗ , let h⊕D be the least consequence relation containing h∪D, and let the environment be
complete. Call h text-refutable if ⊢∗ ̸⊆ h, coherence-refutable if it is A-incoherent, and refutable by
coherence with data if h ⊕ D is A-incoherent for some finite D ⊆ ⊢∗ (accepted human conclusions
used as premises).

Theorem 6.9 (Silent over-generalizations; trivial). [T2 Thm 2.6] (i) h is refutable by coherence
with data iff h ⊕ ⊢∗ is A-incoherent. (ii) The hypotheses refuted in none of the three ways are
exactly U (⊢∗ ) = {h : ⊢∗ ⊆ h, h A-coherent}. (iii) Each h ∈ U (⊢∗ ) \ {⊢∗ } is unsound, and if it is
a possible target, every finite stage of every data stream for ⊢∗ is one for h.

Proof. (i) h ⊕ ⊢∗ is the directed union of the h ⊕ D, and a finite derivation of ⊥ lies in one of
them. (ii) If ⊢∗ ̸⊆ h, h is text-refutable; otherwise h ⊕ D = h for all D ⊆ ⊢∗ . (iii) h ⊋ ⊢∗ accepts
an invalid sequent; data for ⊢∗ are data for h, and A is the same.

Proposition 6.10 (Coherent chains have coherent unions). [T2 Prop 2.7] The union of a chain of
A-coherent finitary consequence relations is one: cut and A ▷ ⊥ involve finitely many sequents,
which lie in one member.

Corollary 6.11 (Fixed designations do not remove limit points). [T2 Cor 2.8, as repaired] Let A be
fixed and known independently of the target. If the possible targets include a strictly increasing
chain h1 ⊊ h2 ⊊ · · · of A-coherent hypotheses together with hω = n hn , no learner, computable
                                                                  S

or not, identifies them in the limit (EX or BC) from text plus coherence information.

Proof. The coherence information is target-independent, so fold it into the learner. A learner for
hω has a locking sequence σ (Blum and Blum, 1975), or its BC analogue; its finite content lies in
some hk , and on a text for hk extending σ the learner conjectures hω forever.

    This is Gold’s limit-point theorem (Gold, 1967). The hypothesis “A fixed” is essential:
designations that are data about the target, as when a problem setter certifies a setup consistent
with the true theory, are genuine negative data.



                                                   49
                                                                                                ′
Proposition 6.12 (Target-dependent designations are negative data; trivial). [T2 Prop 2.8 , repair]
Suppose the environment designates only target-coherent finite contexts, and eventually all of
them. If every hypothesis satisfies classical reductio (Γ ⊢h φ iff Γ ∪ {¬φ} ⊢h ⊥), text plus
designations is an informant, and every countable such class is EX-identifiable by enumeration.
Example: for hn = Cn2 {¬pi : i < n} and hω = Cn2 {¬pi : i ∈ ω}, {pj } is hn -coherent iff j ≥ n,
so the limit point is identified.
    Proof in Appendix E.3. So with fixed designations coherence prunes only over-generalizations
that are not limit points, and positive-data structure (Section 5) decides the rest. Text plus
coherence refutes every wrong hypothesis exactly when U (⊢∗ ) ∩ H = {⊢∗ } (a condition for
refutation, not identifiability: {⊢∗ , h} with h ⊋ ⊢∗ is identifiable from text). Reading: a
coherence loss removes over-acceptance that conflicts; the silent over-generalizations U (⊢∗ ) are
the precise content of “coherent but wrong”, and whether they exist depends on the target, not
on the learner.

6.2    Post-completeness: when coherence suffices
A consequence operator C is structural if σC(X) ⊆ C(σX) for every substitution σ: rules are
uniform schemas, so a learner that generalizes steps to schemas (Section 2.2) is a structural
learner.
Theorem 6.13 (Structural Post-completeness; known). [T2 Thm 3.1] If Fm contains ¬ and one
of ∧, ∨, →, all interpreted by their truth functions, every structural closure operator C ⊇ Cn2 ,
finitary or not, is Cn2 or CFm . The same holds for pure {→}.            Lean: Post.eq_Cn2_or_eq_trivial
Lean: Post.impFrag_postComplete


Proof (main case). Let φ ∈ C(X) \ Cn2 (X), with v[X] = 1, v(φ) = 0. Fix a tautology ⊤0 in
one atom and ⊥0 = ¬⊤0 , and let σv (p) = ⊤0 or ⊥0 as v(p) = 1 or 0. Every Boolean u has
u(σv ψ) = v(ψ), so σv X ⊆ Taut ⊆ C(∅) and σv φ is a contradiction. By structurality σv φ ∈
C(σv X) ⊆ C(C(∅)) = C(∅), hence Fm = Cn2 ({σv φ}) ⊆ C(∅). For {→} see Appendix E.4.

    At the level of theorems this is Post (1921); the consequence-level form is standard too
(Pogorzelski, 1971; Wójcicki, 1988; Pogorzelski and Wojtylak, 2008), and Proposition 3.5 is the
same substitution trick for step sets. One falsifying row of one bad rule yields a derivation of ⊥
from no premises. Without a theorem in the language there is one more extension: in the {∧, ∨}
fragment (Cn2 (∅) = ∅) let Cai (∅) = ∅ and Cai (X) = Fm for X ̸= ∅ (almost inconsistent).
Proposition 6.14 (The theorem-free fragment; presumably folklore). [T2 Prop 3.2] In the {∧, ∨}
language the structural closure operators extending Cn2 are exactly Cn2 , Cai and CFm .
    Proof in Appendix E.4. So there coherence must be enforced on a non-empty designated
context. For sequents D let ⟨D⟩ be the least structural consequence relation containing D, and
for a designated Cn2 -consistent A0 let S(D, A0 ) = {h structural : D ⊆ h, A0 ⊬h ⊥}.
Theorem 6.15 (Coherence pins classical logic). [T2 Thm 3.3, as repaired] Let the language be as
in Theorem 6.13. (a) For D ⊆ Cn2 : S(D, A0 ) = {Cn2 } iff ⟨D⟩ = Cn2 , and a finite D suffices
(with → in the language, Hilbert-style bases with modus ponens as the only rule; without →, by
Rautenberg 1981; Appendix E.4). (b) Let H = (hi ) be any uniformly decidable family of structural
consequence relations with Cn2 ∈ H. The learner outputting the least i such that hi contains the
data so far and A0 ⊬hi ⊥ identifies Cn2 in the limit from every text, with at most i∗ mind changes

                                                  50
(i∗ the least index of Cn2 ), for every ordering of H: no simplicity or minimality bias is needed.
(c) Each assumption is needed: Cn2 + {▷p} (p not in A0 ) is non-structural, A0 -coherent and
contains every text; CFm contains every text; in the theorem-free {∧, ∨} fragment, Cai survives
the empty context.                           Lean: Post.versionSpace_eq_singleton_iff Lean: Post.learner_identifies


Proof idea. (a) Members of S extend ⟨D⟩ = Cn2 , so are Cn2 or CFm , and A0 excludes CFm .
(b) For j < i∗ , either the text refutes hj , or hj ⊋ Cn2 is CFm and A0 rejects it. Details in
Appendix E.4.

  The form of the basis matters: if the ∧ clauses are sequent rules (p ∧ q ▷ p, . . . ) while
modus ponens is the only rule for →, then ▷p → (q → p ∧ q) is underivable (countermodel in
Appendix E.4); giving them as →-axioms repairs this.

Reading. This is a setup of the user’s shape that provably works for formal propositional
mathematics. The algorithm is trivial; the content is Theorem 6.13, whose proof explains
why: an invalid rule has a falsifying row, and substituting ⊤/⊥ along it turns the rule into
“from tautologies infer a contradiction”. A uniform learner cannot hide an invalid schema from
coherence. It must generalize schematically, though: closure under renaming atoms is not enough,
since the least renaming-invariant extension of Cn2 containing p ▷ q is ∅-coherent with theorems
Taut ([L5 Prop 2.5] ; Appendix E.4). In Experiment B (Section 12) a bold learner over classical
natural deduction accepted all 13 valid and refuted all 44 invalid structural candidate schemas,
each by an explicit derivation of ⊢ ⊥ (median 2.5 steps for generalizations of target rules), while
the non-structural ⊢ p survived all eight designated contexts (computed; quick run).

Proposition 6.16 (Theorems versus consequence). [T2 Props 3.4, 3.5, as repaired] For a substitution-
closed theorem set Th let Cadm (X) = {φ : ∀σ(σX ⊆ Th ⇒ σφ ∈ Th)}. (a) Cadm is the largest
structural closure operator with theorems Th; on finite premise sets it is the structural completion
(admissible rules). (b) For Th = Taut in a language as in Theorem 6.13, Cadm = Cn2 (structural
completeness); in the {∧, ∨} fragment Cadm = Cai . (c) For IPC, Cadm ⊋ ⊢IPC (Harrop’s rule
¬p → q ∨ r / (¬p → q) ∨ (¬p → r) is admissible, not derivable; Harrop 1960; Rybakov 1997;
Iemhoff 2001), and the two have the same theorems and the same coherence profile, A ⊢IPC    adm ⊥ iff
A ⊢CPC ⊥.                                                           Lean: Post.le_Cn2_of_empty_eq_Taut


   Proof in Appendix E.4. So a learner can learn the theorems and then take the structural
completion; a learner that is not bold here may settle on X 7→ X ∪ Taut, which has the right
theorems but cannot chain inferences from premises. And no learner fed theorems and coherence
data can tell IPC-derivability from IPC-admissibility.

Theorem 6.17 (Intuitionistic logic: coherence is blind). [T2 Thm 3.6] (a) For every intermediate
logic L and finite A: A ⊢L ⊥ iff A ⊢CPC ⊥. (b) A learner outputting a maximal coherent
structural hypothesis never outputs IPC. (c) There is a strictly increasing chain of finitely
axiomatizable intermediate logics L1 ⊊ L2 ⊊ · · · whose union Lω is intermediate, and no learner
identifies {Lk : k ≤ ω} from text plus coherence information, for any A, even with target-
dependent designations. (d) The conservative learner outputting ⟨Dn ⟩ BC-identifies every finitely
axiomatizable structural target.

Proof idea. (a) is Glivenko’s theorem (Glivenko, 1929). (c) uses Jankov formulas of an infinite
antichain of finite Heyting algebras (Jankov, 1968; Chagrov and Zakharyaschev, 1997); by (a) all
designations are the same for every member, so Corollary 6.11 applies. Appendix E.5.

                                                        51
    So for IPC the work is done by minimality (Section 5), not coherence. Minimality is right
when the target is at the bottom of its coherent version space, coherence plus boldness when it
is at the top; classical logic is at both ends. In Experiment B a bold learner over intuitionistic
natural deduction accepted excluded middle, double-negation elimination, reductio, Peirce’s law,
weak excluded middle and Dummett’s linearity, all IPC-underivable (computed; quick run).

Complete theories. Much of the algebra in a physics calculation is real-closed-field reasoning,
and RCF is complete. Is it pinned like CPC?

Proposition 6.18 (Complete theories: theorems, and rules under ∃-closure). [T2 Prop 3.11, repaired]
Let T be a complete consistent first-order theory. (a) The only consistent closed theory in the
language of T containing T is T ; if T is decidable, the learner of Theorem 6.15(b), run on a
uniformly decidable family of theories containing T , identifies T from a text and the coherence
datum ∅. (b) Add parameters (fresh constants); the target is ⊢T (Γ ⊢T φ iff T ∪ Γ ⊢ φ).
If h ⊇ ⊢T is closed under substituting terms for parameters and under ∃-elimination (from
Γ ⊢h ∃xψ and Γ, ψ(d) ⊢h χ, d fresh, infer Γ ⊢h χ), and ∅ ⊬h ⊥, then h = ⊢T . (c) Without
∃-closure (b) fails, even for a non-empty context. For T = RCF, the least parameter-structural
h2 ⊇ ⊢RCF containing all instances of t3 − 4t + 1 = 0 ▷ t > 1 has h2 (∅) = CnRCF (∅) and
h2 ({c3 − 4c + 1 = 0}) = CnRCF ({c3 − 4c + 1 = 0, c > 1}): coherent on both, yet unsound (the
cubic has three real roots and Galois group S3 ). The context {c3 − 4c + 1 = 0, c < 0} catches it.

Proof idea. (b) An invalid Γ(c̄) ⊢h φ(c̄) gives T ⊢ ∃x̄( Γ ∧ ¬φ); a fresh instance derives ⊥ in h,
                                                      V

and ∃-elimination discharges the parameters one at a time. (c) The rule fires only on terms the
context forces to be roots, and Q(r) contains no other root. Appendix E.6, which also gives a
simpler example (t · t = 2 ▷ t > 0) caught by {c · c = 2}.
    Reading. At the level of theorems, RCF, Presburger arithmetic and Tarski’s geometry are
pinned as cleanly as CPC. At the level the user cares about, rules applied in contexts with
parameters, a step verifier that is merely a set of uniform rules is not pinned by one coherence
datum; it is pinned if its hypotheses are closed under ∃-elimination, a discharge meta-rule rather
than a step, or if the designated contexts are rich enough. (Whether a fixed finite family of
contexts suffices for RCF is open. With sin on R, which defines Z, incompleteness enters; for exp
the question is open: Th(R, exp) is model complete (Wilkie, 1996), and decidable if Schanuel’s
conjecture holds (Macintyre and Wilkie, 1996).) Designation is soundness-critical in the other
direction too: designating an idealization together with the background it contradicts forces
every structural coherent learner to lose a classical schema everywhere (Proposition 11.10), which
is the formal content of the user’s air-pressure worry and the subject of Section 11.

6.3   Arithmetic: coherence is Popperian
Hypotheses are r.e. theories T ⊇ PA, world feedback is a ∆0 -oracle, and N is the intended model;
Q is Robinson arithmetic.

Lemma 6.19 (∆0 feedback is subsumed by coherence; standard). [T2 Lemma 3.7] A consistent
T ⊇ Q proves every true ∆0 sentence and no false one, so no ∆0 feedback refutes it.

Proof. Q is Σ1 -complete (Boolos et al., 2007); if T proved a false δ it would also prove ¬δ.

Theorem 6.20 (No maximal r.e. hypothesis; cited). [T2 Thm 3.8, as repaired] Every consistent r.e.
T ⊇ Q has a ρ with T + ρ and T + ¬ρ consistent (Gödel, 1931; Rosser, 1936; Tarski et al., 1953).

                                                52
     So a bold learner with r.e. hypotheses and A = {∅} has no maximal choice. (If A contains all
finite sets of true sentences, T is A-coherent iff T ⊆ Th(N), and at most one refinement survives.)

Theorem 6.21 (Turing-chain impossibility). [T2 Thm 3.9, as repaired] Let T0 = PA, Tk+1 = Tk +
Con(Tk ), Tω = k Tk (Turing, 1939; Feferman, 1962), and let A be any fixed family of finite sets
               S

of true sentences. No learner identifies a class containing {Tk : k ≤ ω} from text, A-coherence
and a ∆0 -oracle.

Proof. All Tk are sound, so all side information is target-independent; the chain is strict by
Gödel II; apply Corollary 6.11.

    The Σ1 -unsound Tk +¬Con(Tk ) are natural hypotheses: consistent, unrefuted by ∆0 data, and
refuted by coherence only relative to accepted premises proving Con(Tk ). Restricting designations
to true contexts is essential: if {¬Con(Tk )} could be designated as data about the target, the
chain would be identifiable (Proposition 6.12).

Theorem 6.22 (Coherence as a Π1 -truth detector; the Σ2 barrier). [T2 Thm 3.10, as repaired] (a)
For Π1 π, PA + π is consistent iff π is true. (b) For Σ1 σ, truth implies consistency of PA + σ,
not conversely (σ = ¬Con(PA)). (c) The Popperian learner (“true” on a Π1 sentence until PA
refutes it; “false” on a Σ1 sentence until PA proves it) converges to the truth on Σ1 ∪ Π1 with
at most one mind change per sentence. (d) The naively bold learner (accept φn iff consistent
with PA plus the earlier accepted sentences) converges to a Lindenbaum completion that depends
on the enumeration, and on every enumeration beginning with ¬Con(PA) it is Σ1 -unsound. (e)
Every computable learner using a computable text, coherence searches and a ∆0 -oracle fails to
converge to the truth value of some Σ2 sentence.

Proof idea. (a) A false Π1 sentence has a true, hence PA-provable, Σ1 negation. (e) Convergent
computable guesses define a ∆2 set (Shoenfield, 1959), and the true Σ2 sentences are Σ2 -complete.
Appendix E.7.
    This is Popper’s asymmetry in its standard learning-theoretic form (Putnam, 1965; Gold,
1965; Kelly, 1996), read for coherence-learners. The same boundary appears for credences, in a
self-contained form of a theorem of Sawin and Demski (2013). Quantifier blocks may be empty,
so Π1 sentences also count as Π2 and Σ2 .

Theorem 6.23 (The coherence/Π1 /Π2 trilemma). [L3 Thm D, as repaired] Let Pt (φ) ∈ Q ∩ [0, 1] be
computable uniformly in t, φ, with (i) limt Pt (π) = 1 for every true Π1 π, and (ii) lim supt (Pt (π) +
Pt (φ)) ≤ 1 whenever π is Π1 and PA ⊢ π → ¬φ. Then infinitely many true Π2 sentences have
lim inf t Pt (φ) = 0.

Proof. A false Π2 φ = ∀x∃yR has a true Π1 refuter ∀y¬R(x̄0 , y), so lim supt Pt (φ) = 0 by (i),
(ii). Thus {φ ∈ Π2 : lim inf t Pt (φ) > 0}, a Σ2 set, contains no false Π2 sentence; if it contained all
but finitely many true ones, the Π2 -complete set of true Π2 sentences would be Σ2 , contradicting
Post’s hierarchy theorem.

    So one can have at most three of: computability, weak coherence, Π1 -convergence, and
non-dogmatism on true Π2 sentences; logical induction drops Π1 -convergence. Coherence does
not, however, confine credences to ∆2 :
                                                               ′
Proposition 6.24 (What weak coherence costs). [L3 Thm D , as repaired] Say computable credences
gradually verify C if Pt (φ) → 1 iff φ is true, for φ ∈ C. (a) Convergent credences, coherent

                                                  53
or not, have a Π2 success set, so never gradually verify the Σ2 sentences. (b) Credences as in
Theorem 6.23(ii) never gradually verify all Π2 sentences. (c) Some computable credences, weakly
coherent for all pairs, gradually verify the Σ2 sentences (necessarily without converging on all of
them).

    Proof in Appendix E.8. Incoherent computable credences can gradually verify the Π2 sentences
convergently (L3 Thm G(f), a standard construction given there as a sketch); so among convergent
credences, and among the classes Σ1 , Π1 , B(Σ1 ), Σ2 , Π2 , Π3 of that success table, weak coherence
costs exactly the Π2 class, and part of what incoherent credences reach beyond that is paid for
by giving up convergence, not coherence.

Reading. In arithmetic, computation adds nothing beyond coherence with a Σ1 -complete
base. What matters is how boldness is applied: boldly on universal claims, which coherence can
refute, cautiously on existential ones, which need a witness. Beyond ∆2 no computable channel
decides truth in the limit (Theorem 6.22(e)); only the weaker gradual verification, with credence
tending to 1 exactly on truths, reaches further (Proposition 6.24). The Gödel–Rosser alternatives
are a permanent residue, removed only by accepting stronger principles (reflection, Con(PA), a
stronger theory): a justification that is not a PA-proof (Section 13).

6.4    Carnap’s problem is Gold’s problem
Under inferentialism, learning valid inferences should be learning meanings. Carnap (1943)
observed that the classical rules do not fix the truth tables: the all-true valuation, and “true iff
tautologous”, respect every classical inference.

Definition 6.25 (Meanings). [T2 §4.1] A valuation is any v : Fm → {0, 1}, identified with its
true-set in Cantor space 2Fm ; a meaning is a set V of valuations; BV is the set of Boolean ones.
For finite Γ, ∆, Γ |=1V φ iff every v ∈ V with v[Γ] = 1 has v(φ) = 1, and Γ |=m
                                                                              V ∆ iff no v ∈ V has
v[Γ] = 1, v[∆] = 0. Val(S) is the set of valuations satisfying the sequents S; V ∩ closes V under
intersections of true-sets, including the empty one v⊤ ≡ 1; vTaut is the characteristic function of
Taut. V is structural if v ∈ V implies v ◦ σ ∈ V for all substitutions σ.

    The sequent Γ ▷ ∆ excludes exactly the clopen set of valuations realizing the position [Γ : ∆].
So inference data are negative data about valuations, while coherence certifications (“[A : D] is in
bounds”) and world feedback are positive data: Restall’s reading of consequence (Restall, 2005)
turned into a statement about data.

Lemma 6.26 (Duality). [T2 Lemma 4.1] (a) Val(|=m   V ) = V . (b) For closed V , CV (X) = {v ∈ V :
                                                                                           T

X ⊆ v} is finitary and the valuations satisfying every valid single-conclusion sequent form V ∩ .
(c) BV∩ is the set of characteristic functions of classical theories, including Fm.    Lean: Val_mrel
Lean: lemma_4_1_b Lean: intClosure_BV_eq


Theorem 6.27 (Carnap’s problem in learning form). [T2 Thm 4.2, as repaired] |=1BV = |=1W whenever
BV ⊆ W ⊆ BV∩ . So no data that are a function of the single-conclusion relation (text, informant,
or metarules with discharge read globally, as closure conditions) distinguish BV from W . The
non-Boolean members of BV∩ are the vT for theories T that are not maximal consistent (including
T = Fm): v⊤ violates the table for ¬; vTaut and every non-maximal consistent vT violate those
for ¬, ∨, →; all respect ∧. BV∩ is closed and structural.                          Lean: carnap_single

Proof. By Lemma 6.26(b) every w ∈ W satisfies |=1BV , and W ⊇ BV gives the converse.

Appendix E.9.

   Under the local reading of rule validity (Garson, 2013), →I does exclude vTaut , but only by
imposing the two-conclusion sequent ▷φ, φ → ψ. The right measure is the number of denials.
Theorem 6.28 (Denial-rank trichotomy). [T2 Thm 4.3] With ¬ in the language, let the denial
rank d(v) be the least |∆| over classically valid Γ ▷ ∆ (finite) that v violates (∞ if none). Then
d(v) = 0 iff v’s true-set is inconsistent; d(v) = 1 iff it is consistent but not closed; d(v) = 2 iff it
is a consistent, closed, non-maximal theory; d(v) = ∞ iff v ∈ BV.               Lean: denialRank_eq_two_iff

Proof. d = 0 is a finite inconsistent subset; for consistent v, d ≤ 1 says some Γ ⊆ v entails some
φ∈/ v; a consistent closed non-maximal T omits φ and ¬φ, violating ▷φ, ¬φ; maximal theories
are Boolean.


Reading. “Coherence” covers two kinds of data (Table 6): certifications that a position is in
bounds (positive about V ), and 0-denial constraints such as p, ¬p▷ (negative about V ), which
remove v⊤ . Neither removes vTaut . A learner trained on imitation plus a coherence loss, in
either sense, is provably free to settle on gappy valuations: consistent, inferentially competent,
never committing on open questions. These are van Fraassen’s supervaluations (van Fraassen,
1966), the honest semantics of “settled by the premises”. Pinning classical meaning needs a loss
with two denials. Bilateral signed sequents (Smiley, 1996; Rumfitt, 2000) carry the same rank-2
information as multiple conclusions. A testable prediction for language-model training: imitation
plus a contradiction penalty yields consistent but noncommittal reasoners.
    With rank-2 data finitely many data suffice. Let TT be the eleven truth-table schemata:
φ, ¬φ▷ and ▷φ, ¬φ; φ ∧ ψ ▷ φ, φ ∧ ψ ▷ ψ, φ, ψ ▷ φ ∧ ψ; φ ▷ φ ∨ ψ, ψ ▷ φ ∨ ψ, φ ∨ ψ ▷ φ, ψ;
▷φ, φ → ψ, ψ ▷ φ → ψ, φ, φ → ψ ▷ ψ. TT(p, q) are their instances in distinct atoms.
Theorem 6.29 (Bilateral determinacy with a twelve-datum tell-tale). [T2 Thm 4.4, as repaired] Let
the language be {¬, ∧, ∨, →} (with ⊥, ⊤ add ⊥▷ and ▷⊤; the Lean version has 13 + 1 data). (a)
Val(TT) = BV, which is closed. (b) If V is structural, every v ∈ V satisfies the eleven sequents
TT(p, q), and V = ̸ ∅ (one coherence datum), then V = BV. (c) So among structural meanings
BV is identified finitely by twelve data; without the coherence datum V = ∅ survives, the Gold
over-generalization. (d) Without structurality BV is not identifiable in the limit at all, even with
the coherence datum: every finite set of valid sequents is valid in a closed meaning BV ∪ {vχ′ }, vχ′
a Boolean valuation flipped at a formula χ the set does not mention. And the dense non-structural
BV \ {u0 } has the same valid sequents and in-bounds positions as BV.            Lean: structural_eq_BV
Lean: BV_not_BC_learnable

Proof idea. (a) Each schema enforces one table row. (b) v satisfies σ(s) iff v ◦ σ ∈ V satisfies s;
and any Boolean u is v ◦ σ for σ sending atoms to p0 ∨ ¬p0 or its negation. (d) No tell-tale, then

    The three two-conclusion schemata in TT are exactly the rank-2 data that vTaut violates.
Brute force over the sixteen formulas of depth ≤ 1 in two atoms confirms (a), Theorem 6.27 and
all 216 denial ranks; the T2 verifier found, over all structural meanings of two- and three-element
matrices, no non-Boolean survivor of the twelve data and each datum necessary (computed). A
second remedy restricts the hypothesis class, as in Gold’s setting:
Proposition 6.30 (A compositional prior suffices). [T2 Prop 4.5] Exactly one two-element matrix
with designated value 1 validates explosion, ¬¬p ▷ p, p ▷ ¬¬p, the three ∧ rules, ∨-introduction,
disjunctive syllogism, modus ponens, q ▷ p → q and ¬p ▷ p → q: the standard one.
    Proof in Appendix E.9; compare Bonnay and Westerståhl (2016), whose compositionality
is more general. World feedback alone fixes nothing, since the actual valuation is Boolean and
never refutes V ⊇ BV; under the compositional prior it identifies the tables once every row has
been observed [T2 Prop 4.6] .

Reading. Carnap’s problem is a semantic twin of Gold’s only in part (Table 7). Gold’s trivial
logic (V = ∅) is removed by a coherence certification, Carnap’s v⊤ by a 0-denial sequent, and
vTaut , the Horn blindness of single-conclusion data, by neither. So imitation plus coherence fixes
inferential role, but not classical truth conditions. That does not matter for deriving; it matters
for interpreting world feedback (“A is false” licenses ¬A only through the bilateral bridge) and
for “verification from truth”. And rules can be identifiable when truths are not: Theorem 6.29
identifies classical meaning finitely, while no computable learner decides arithmetical truth
(Theorem 6.22(e)).

6.5    Tonk, harmony and conservativity
Prior (1960)’s tonk (φ ▷ φ tonk ψ, φ tonk ψ ▷ ψ) is the paradigm of a rule search exploits
(Theorem 3.2); Belnap (1962) demanded that new rules be conservative.
Proposition 6.31 (Tonk). [T2 Prop 5.1] Under cut, tonk gives φ ⊢ ψ for all φ, ψ, and coherence
refutes it in two steps (a ▷ a tonk ⊥ ▷ ⊥) if A has a non-empty context or the logic has a theorem.
In a theorem-free base with A = {∅} it survives: classical {∧, ∨} plus tonk is Cai . Without cut
tonk need not trivialize (Cook, 2005; Ripley, 2015).

                                                  56
Proof. One cut gives the first claim. In the theorem-free case every rule has premises, so nothing
is derivable from ∅, while from any premise tonk yields everything.

     Transitivity is double-edged: it lets one bad rule poison everything, and it lets coherence
catch it. In Experiment B a bold learner offered the two tonk rules in either order accepted the
first and rejected the second (each alone has a truth-table reading): boldness is order-dependent
when individually coherent rules are jointly incoherent (computed; quick run).
Proposition 6.32 (Coherence is not conservativity). [T2 Prop 5.2, as repaired] Intuitionistic → plus
¬-introduction, ¬-elimination and ¬¬-elimination is the {→, ¬} fragment of CPC: coherent, but
not conservative over IPC→ (it proves Peirce’s law). ¬¬-elimination alone would be conservative.
Conservativity implies coherence when coherence is non-triviality on the old vocabulary, or ⊥
with ex falso is in the base, but not in general (IPC→ + {▷⊥}, with ⊥ new and without ex falso).
     Proof in Appendix E.10. So Belnap’s learner rejects classical negation over intuitionistic posi-
tive logic, and the coherence learner accepts it. Harmony with normalization gives conservativity
(Prawitz, 1965; Dummett, 1991), but local harmony alone does not: Read’s bullet (Read, 2000),
introduced from a derivation of ⊥ from [•] and eliminated from •, • to ⊥, has local reductions
yet derives ⊥ from nothing. Over a Post-complete base the two notions coincide [L5 Cor 2.4] : if
C ′ is structural on L′ ⊇ L with C ′ |L ⊇ Cn2 (L as in Theorem 6.13), then C ′ |L is Cn2 or CFm ,
so C ′ is conservative iff it is coherent on one classically consistent context. Belnap’s existence
condition is, over classical logic, non-triviality; harmony matters only over sub-classical bases.
Theorem 6.33 (Coherence is Π1 , conservativity Π2 ). [T2 Thm 5.3] For finite rule systems (ele-
mentary formal systems; Post 1943; Smullyan 1961): (a) {R : R ⊬ ⊥} is Π1 -complete; (b) for
some fixed finite base B, {N : B + N conservative over B} is Π2 -complete; (c) so coherence is
decidable in the limit with one mind change, while conservativity is not decidable in the limit by
any computable learner, only refutable in the limit (Kelly, 1996); (d) for decidable B (e.g. CPC,
IPC) conservativity is Π1 .
Proof idea. (a) Simulate φe (e) by rules and add “halt ▷⊥”. (b) A new symbol c with ▷c ē and
c y, Num(x) ▷ T (x, y) over a universal base is conservative iff φe is total. Appendix E.10.
     Reading. A coherence filter can be built into a convergent learner (Theorem 6.15(b)); a
conservativity filter cannot in general (over the base of (b)). Coherence plus decidable local
checks such as harmony are the learner-friendly norms. Where non-conservativity is the point,
as for theoretical terms in physics, a concept whose rules smuggle in a substantive inference
(Dummett’s Boche) is coherent, and only world feedback can test it.

6.6    Fallacies and the Kripkensteinian residue
A learner with a generalization operator G (e.g. structural closure of least generalizations) turns
finitely many fallacious human steps F into rules G(F ). Let h∗ be the target consequence relation.
Theorem 6.34 (Elimination criterion; trivial). [T2 Thm 6.1] Coherence (with data) eventually
refutes the generalized fallacy iff h∗ ⊕ G(F ) is A-incoherent; for OH the refutation is charged
to the budget of Theorem 6.5. If h∗ is sound for a class of intended structures and each A ∈ A
holds in one that also validates G(F ), the fallacy survives.
Proof. h∗ ⊕ G(F ) contains every positive datum; apply Theorem 6.9. A structure validating h∗ ,
G(F ) and A shows coherence on A.

                                                 57
                    fallacy                    rule               witness
                    affirming the consequent   q, p → q / p       p := ⊥, q := ⊤
                    denying the antecedent     ¬p, p → q / ¬q     p := ⊥, q := ⊤
                    conversion                 p→q /q→p           p := ⊥, q := ⊤
                    illicit contraposition     p → q / ¬p → ¬q    p := ⊥, q := ⊤
                    “or” read as “xor”         p ∨ q, p / ¬q      p := q := ⊤

         Table 8: Propositional fallacies and their Post witnesses (computed; T2-checks).


Corollary 6.35 (All structural propositional fallacies die, quickly). [T2 Cor 6.2] If G is structural
closure and h∗ ⊇ Cn2 is structural and coherent on a designated context (so h∗ = Cn2 ), every
classically invalid rule is eliminated: substituting ⊤/⊥ along a falsifying row makes its premises
tautologies and its conclusion a contradiction (Table 8). Given the row, the ⊥-derivation has
polynomial size in any Frege system (proof sketch for the size bound); finding the row is an NP
search.

Proof. h∗ ⊕ G(F ) is a structural proper extension of Cn2 , hence CFm (Theorem 6.13).

    Experiment B shows the negative-data role end to end (computed; quick run). From 50 simulated
human proofs with systematic fallacies (affirming the consequent, denying the antecedent, and
an illegitimate discharge that was never learned) and 5% corrupted steps, imitation ended with
22 unsound active rules, 13 of them tonk-like, and a budgeted adversarial prover derived ⊢ ⊥
and all 34 of its false goals. Coherence pruning on five designated contexts, with blame assigned
by a minimum-weight hitting set over the bags found, removed every unsound rule and the two
fallacies; all 13 target rules were recovered (exactly or with a stronger guard), completeness
matched the target calculus under the same prover budget, and the adversary proved none of
its goals. From only 10 proofs affirming the consequent survived, plausibly because the learned
calculus lacked the rules needed to derive the contradiction: coherence is only as strong as the
calculus that searches for it. Outside a Post-complete target, fallacies can survive.

Example 6.36 (Survivors). [T2 §6, as repaired] (1) Quantifier swap ∀x∃yR / ∃y∀xR is sound in
one-element structures, hence ∅-coherent; its alternative meaning is “the domain is a singleton”,
and any designated context with 0 ̸= 1 kills it. (2) The gambler’s fallacy is incoherent with
independence, but coherent without it: an anti-persistent stationary Markov chain with switching
probability 0.6 has fair marginals and validates it. Only frequencies or an accepted independence
premise remove it. (3) Base-rate neglect as a uniform schema P (E|H) = a / P (H|E) = a yields
P (H|E) + P (¬H|E) = 1.8 when P (E|H) = P (E|¬H) = 0.9, and dies with additivity. (4)
Conditional perfection (from q and a law p → q ∈ K, infer p; Geis and Zwicky 1971) is coherent
whenever the per-law converse {q → p : (p → q) ∈ K} is consistent with K ∪ A. This is sufficient,
not necessary (K = {p1 → q1 , p2 → q2 }, A = {q1 ∨ q2 , ¬p1 , ¬p2 }: the converse is inconsistent
with K ∪ A, yet neither qi is derivable, so the step rule never fires and the fallacy survives, for
step-set hypotheses without proof by cases). It dies when two exclusive laws share a consequent
(rain → wet, sprinkler → wet, ¬(rain ∧ sprinkler), A = {wet}). The per-law converse is not
Clark’s completion (Clark, 1978), which is consistent there.

Proposition 6.37 (Without uniformity, coherence is toothless; trivial). [T2 Prop 6.3] If h∗ ⊕ F
is A-coherent, the non-structural h∗ ⊕ F survives forever; e.g. Cn2 + {▷p17 }, the “quus” of
propositional logic, when every designated context is consistent with p17 .

                                                 58
Theorem 6.38 (The Kripkensteinian residue). [T2 Thm 6.4, as repaired] AltG (h∗ , A) = {h G-closed :
h ⊇ h∗ , h A-coherent} is, in the limit of complete data, the set of G-closed hypotheses never
refuted by positive data and coherence (Theorem 6.9). (i) G structural, h∗ = Cn2 , A =        ̸ ∅:
Alt = {Cn2 }. (ii) G structural, h∗ = IPC, A a non-empty family of classically consistent contexts:
Alt = {h structural : IPC ⊆ h ⊆ CPC}, all intermediate logics. (iii) G deductive closure, h∗ = PA,
A = {∅}: Alt is the set of all consistent closed extensions of PA, e.g. PA + ¬Con(PA) and
its non-standard completions. Designating all finite sets of true sentences removes exactly the
unsound ones; the sound Tk of Theorem 6.21 remain, and cannot be discriminated.
Proof idea. (i) is Theorem 6.13. For (ii) ⊆, a classically invalid sequent in a structural h ⊇ IPC
becomes, under σv , an IPC-provable premise set with an IPC-refutable conclusion. Appendix E.11.

Division of labour. Community practice (positive data) eliminates under-generalizations.
Uniformity (structurality, least generalization) eliminates gerrymanders such as Proposition 6.37;
it is vocabulary-relative (Goodman’s point), and it converts an instance-level fallacy into a
schema that coherence can refute. Coherence eliminates uniform alternatives that conflict with
accepted uniform rules: tonk; affirming the consequent; “quus” as a second rule for + beside
x + Sy = S(x + y). Nothing in the setup eliminates coherent uniform alternatives that agree with
all data. For classical propositional logic there are none; for arithmetic they are the Gödel–Rosser
and non-standard alternatives, a precise mathematical counterpart of Kripkenstein for arithmetic
(Kripke, 1982), removable only by accepting reflection or stronger theories on other grounds
(Section 13).

6.7    A remark on eliminative versus confirmational coherence
Coherentist epistemology has impossibility results: no informative coherence measure is truth-
conducive ceteris paribus (Bovens and Hartmann, 2003b,a; Olsson, 2005a,b). They do not
threaten the use of coherence in this section, and they do refute a tempting other use [L11 §3.4] .
Remark 6.39 (Three uses of coherence). (U1) Eliminative: “h derives ⊥ from a context certified
consistent, so h has an invalid rule.” This is modus tollens, not a measure. Conditioning a
prior on “h is A-coherent” multiplies every coherent hypothesis by 1/P(coherent): under truthful
designation the target’s posterior weakly rises and odds among coherent rivals are unchanged
(L11 TC4). The impossibility theorems, about degrees of coherence of consistent information
sets, do not apply; the prices are Theorem 6.9 and the need for truthful designation. (U2)
Probabilistic consistency of credences: de Finetti constraints, satisfied by the noncommittal
assignment, the credal analogue of gappy valuations. (U3) Agreement as evidence: annotators or
sampled derivations agree. This is the witness scenario of Lewis (1946) that the impossibility
theorems address, and they bite in full.
Remark 6.40 (Consensus does not amplify under a common cause). [L11 TC3] Let m annotators
judge a step. With probability π > 0, independently of validity, they all copy one shared judgment
that says “valid” with probability a for valid and b > 0 for invalid steps; otherwise they judge
independently with rates p > q. Then
                         P(all say valid | valid)    πa + (1 − π)pm    1
                                                   =               m
                                                                     ≤
                        P(all say valid | invalid)   πb + (1 − π)q     πb
for every m, against (p/q)m when π = 0. So unanimity among annotators sharing an error source,
or self-consistency across samples of one model, moves the odds by a bounded factor. Human

                                                59
errors in mathematics are often systematic in this sense, which is why corroboration must come
from channels whose errors are independent of the human ones, Daniels’ independence constraint
(Daniels, 1979).
    The eliminative use has a quantitative safety guarantee whose converse shows what it depends
on. Let W ⊆ {0, 1}S be the worlds allowed by constraints on a finite set S of sentences, K their
convex hull, and score credences x in world w by ∥x − w∥2 .
                                                                                     ′
Lemma 6.41 (Coherentizing is safe iff the constraints are sound). [L3 Lemma E , as repaired] For
x∈/ K with Euclidean projection x∗ onto K, every w ∈ K has ∥x − w∥2 ≥ ∥x − x∗ ∥2 + ∥x∗ − w∥2 .
Conversely, if the actual world’s indicator v is not in W, then v ∈
                                                                  / K, and projecting x = v raises
the actual loss from 0 to ∥v − v ∗ ∥2 > 0.

Proof. ⟨x − x∗ , w − x∗ ⟩ ≤ 0. For the converse, a 0/1 vector is an extreme point of the cube, so it
lies in the convex hull of 0/1 vectors only if it is one of them.

     This is the accuracy-dominance argument of de Finetti (1974) and Joyce (1998) (Bregman
projections for other proper scores; Predd et al. 2009). It belongs to the projection, not to
arbitrary arbitrage removal: for x = (0.6, 0.6) on {φ, ¬φ}, moving to (1, 0) costs 2.0 in world
(0, 1), against 0.52 before and 0.5 for the projection. The converse is the probabilistic form of
the air-pressure worry: coherence pressure from an idealized context the world violates, or from
untrusted learned rules, can make credences strictly worse.

Reading. The setup’s use of coherence is eliminative, hence immune to the coherentist impossi-
bility theorems, but weak: it never confirms, it only removes what conflicts. Corroboration must
come from channels with independent errors: computation, randomized evaluation, measurement
(Section 2.5). Where the target is coherence-pinned, uniqueness of the coherent extension, not
truth-conduciveness of coherence, does the work. Section 13 reads this division as vindicated
wide reflective equilibrium (Goodman, 1955; Daniels, 1979).


7    An end-to-end theorem for formal mathematics: the two-tier
     learner
The user’s first success criterion is “a setup of this shape that provably works for formal math”:
imitate human inferences, then condition on coherence and on occasional feedback from the world.
Sections 4 to 6 supply the parts. The natural way to assemble them is a statement of roughly
this form. For every target calculus with finite anchors, human data with sporadic noise below
the margin and a systematic fallacy set F , and every adaptive prover: (i) a cautious assertion
tier is sound at all times with probability at least 1 − δ; (ii) the number of detected incoherences
is at most log2 (1/w∗ ) plus a noise cost; (iii) after N human steps the tier equals the target plus
the fallacies coherent with it; and (iv) that residue is empty for classical propositional logic and
for complete decidable theories.
    This statement is false as stated, and the way it fails is the main finding of this section. Three
corrections are needed, and each is forced by a matching lower bound.
1. Caution is relative to a class, and fallacies break realizability. Positive data cannot tell a
   systematic fallacy from a rule (Corollary 5.20), so the realizable object is the practice, not the
   target. A tier that is cautious over the positive-data version space asserts every frequent fallacy


                                                 60
   (Proposition 7.5). A tier that is cautious over hypotheses allowed to declare practice rules
   fallacious asserts nothing (Proposition 7.6). What works is presumption of validity applied
   only after an audit, with abstention until then, and the waiting is necessary (Theorem 7.20).
2. Coherence condemns sets, and the blame problem is real. The practice schemas that can be
   soundly condemned as schemas are exactly those in the union of the minimal conflicts, which
   is the hitting-set duality of model-based diagnosis (Lemma 7.8). At stable depth this collateral
   damage is unavoidable for every sound learner (Theorem 7.21). The extremal witness is almost
   too clean: in the practice {modus ponens, affirming the consequent} the rival diagnosis is the
   hypothesis that “→” means “←”. World feedback with descent, or a single denial, turns bags
   into singletons (Lemma 7.9).
3. Soundness must be relativized to refutation size. No computable learner is both sound at all
   times relative to full coherence and eventually complete (Theorem 7.22).
With these corrections the theorem holds (Theorem 7.14). We call the resulting learner TTL, the
two-tier learner: a cautious, audited assertion tier and a bold sandbox that a red team attacks.
    Depth, honestly. Most individual steps are trivial once set up, or are results of Sections 3
to 6 transported. Lemma 7.8 is Reiter’s diagnosis theory, Lemma 7.9 is Shapiro’s contradiction
backtracing, Theorem 7.20 is a standard two-point rare-event bound, and Theorem 7.22 is a
routine reduction. What is new is diagnostic: where the natural statement breaks (Propositions 7.5
and 7.6); the audit principle; the blame theorem with its →/← witness and its stable-depth
form; the depth relativization; the observation that world feedback earns its keep by localization,
not refutation; and the assembly into one statement with explicit constants. None of it is deep
mathematics. Its value is that it pins down, with matching lower bounds, what “provably works
for formal math” can and cannot mean. All results come from theory thread T7, which went
through two rounds of adversarial verification; several statements below are the repaired ones,
and we say so where it matters. The second-round repairs (assumption (OE) in Theorem 7.20,
the floor bound in Theorem 7.29(e) and the size convention of Definition 7.2) were checked by
their author and by computation, not by a further independent referee.

7.1   Set-up: practice, evaluation, refutations
We use the practice of Definition 2.10: a target calculus Σ∗ of genuine rules, a finite set F of
systematic fallacies, the practice ΣP = Σ∗ ⊔ F with |ΣP | ≤ K, and i.i.d. human steps tagged
by practice schemas, with sporadic noise. Fallacy tags are latent but distinct. For tag i write
βi = πi (1 − ai ) and αi = πi ai , let ρi , ci be the variability and witness count of Theorem 5.7
(ρi = ci = 1 for ground rules, so ci ≥ 1 for every tag), and let ∆i = (ρi βi − αi )/2 be the margin
of Theorem 5.15.
Assumption 7.1 ((Floor)). [T7 §1.2, Def 1.2] Constants ∆ > 0, c ≥ 1, K and ᾱi ≥ αi are known,
with |ΣP | ≤ K, ci ≤ c and (ρi βi − ᾱi )/2 ≥ ∆ for every tag i, fallacy tags included.
    The evaluation sources are those of Definition 2.13: a finite family A of designated positions
[A : D], a world W : JW → {0, 1}, and trusted steps Ttrust (for instance, first-order logic when
only non-logical axioms are learned). Each position induces the partial evaluation e[A:D] of (WS)
(Assumption 2.14): value 1 on A and on W -true judgments, value 0 on D ∪ {⊥} and on W -false
ones. When JW =   ̸ ∅, the empty position [∅ : ∅] is designated. A step is falsified under e if its
premises are e-true and its conclusion e-false. (WS) is assumed for every designated position
unless stated otherwise.

                                                61
Definition 7.2 (Derivations, refutations, conflicts, oracle). [T7 Def 1.5, size convention of Round 2]
 (a) A derivation is a finite sequence of distinct judgments, each a leaf or the conclusion of one
     designated step whose premises occur earlier. Its size |π| is the total number of symbols in
     its distinct judgments. A tree derivation (Definition 2.2) compresses to such a sequence,
     using only its steps and leaves and no larger: keep, for each judgment, the step at an
     occurrence of least height, and order the judgments by that height.
(b) For a finite schema set B (or a step set S) a d-refutation is a derivation of size at most d,
    relative to some [A : D] ∈ A, whose leaves have e-value 1, whose steps lie in RB ∪ Ttrust
    (resp. S ∪ Ttrust ), and whose conclusion has e-value 0. This is Definition 2.20 with the size
    convention made explicit. A single falsified instance is a one-step refutation. B is d-clean if
    it has no d-refutation, and clean at every depth if it is d-clean for all d.
 (c) Conf d = {C ⊆ ΣP : C is not d-clean} is upward closed. Its minimal members Cd are the
     minimal d-conflicts, and Cd (B) denotes those contained in B. Since ΣP is finite there is d0
     with Conf d = Conf d0 for d ≥ d0 ; we write d = ∞ for these stable values.
(d) The refutation oracle Ref d (B) returns a d-refutation of B or NONE, deterministically. By
    default it returns the lexicographically least among the smallest refutations. The prefer-
    unblocked variant returns one whose descent (Lemma 7.9) is unblocked if one exists, then
    the smallest, then the lexicographically least.

    Ref d is computable by brute force when instance membership and W are decidable and there
are finitely many derivations of size at most d up to renaming the symbols that occur in neither
A nor B and on which W does not depend. Its cost is exp(O(d)), and it is accounted separately
from samples. As d → ∞ refutation search is only semi-decidable (Theorem 6.33).
                                                                                              (d)
Definition 7.3 (Residue, survivors, collateral). [T7 Def 1.6] The depth-d residue is Fres = {τ ∈
                                                                   (d)
F : Σ∗ ∪ {τ } is d-clean} (Definition 2.20), and Fres = d Fres ; for τ ∈ F , dτ = min{d :
                                                              T

Σ∗ ∪{τ } is not d-clean}. The survivors are Fdsurv = F \ Cd , and the collateral is Colld = Σ∗ ∩ Cd ,
                                                        S                                       S

the genuine rules lying in some minimal conflict; Colld (B) = Σ∗ ∩ Cd (B). Singleton blame
                                                                        S

(SBd ) holds if every member of Cd is a singleton.

    The residue is the finite-depth version of the Kripkensteinian residue of Theorem 6.38: the
fallacies that are coherent with the target on the designated positions, and world-adequate, up to
size d. Provers are as in Definition 2.18: adaptive, unbounded, and knowing Σ∗ , F , the learner’s
code and the history, but not future data. Uniform δ-soundness relative to a step set S † is that
of Definition 2.19; TTL uses no coins, so its event G depends only on the human data.

Lemma 7.4 (Refutational evidence is one-sided and target-blind; trivial). [T7 Lemma 2.1 and §1.3, as repaired]
Assume (WS). (a) Every step set S ⊆ Sound(R∗ ) is clean at every depth; in particular every
subset of Σ∗ is. (b) Every member of Conf d contains a fallacy. (c) Conf d and every answer of
Ref d are functions of (A, W, Ttrust , d) and the queried set alone; they do not depend on which
schemas are genuine. (d) If e[A:D] is well defined, then (WS) holds for [A : D] iff Σ∗ is clean at
every depth relative to [A : D]. The same holds with any calculus Σ in place of Σ∗ .

Proof idea. The total valuation V of (WS) extends e and preserves truth along R∗ ∪ Ttrust , hence
along Sound(R∗ ); so every node of a derivation from e-true leaves is V -true, and its conclusion is
not e-false. For (d,⇐) take V to be the indicator of ClR∗ ∪Ttrust (A ∪ W −1 (1)). Appendix F.1.


                                                   62
   So refutations can never condemn a subset of the target, and two targets with the same
practice receive identical refutational evidence on every query. Coherence and the world can
say that a set of practice rules contains a fallacy; they can never say that a rule is genuine.
Everything positive that is ever learned about a rule comes from imitation.

7.2    Why the naive two-tier claim is false
Proposition 7.5 (Caution over practice asserts the fallacies; trivial). [T7 Prop 2.2, as repaired] Let
the positive-data tier be the per-tag trimmed version-space verifier (Theorem 5.14) over the
practice tags. On the event of Theorem 5.15 its accepted set is exactly RP = R∗ ∪ RF . So it
accepts an instance outside Sound(R∗ ) of every τ ∈ F , and an adaptive prover gets that instance
accepted by querying it. In CPC with a complete Σ∗ and a pure τ , the prover can query the closed
⊤/⊥ instance of τ with true premises and a false conclusion (Lemma 7.24), and that single step
makes every formula derivable from ∅.

Proof. Theorem 5.15 applies tag by tag, and fallacy tags are tags like any other. In the CPC
case, Σ∗ derives the closed true premises and the negation of the closed false conclusion j, both
tautologies; the step adds j, and then ⊥ and everything follow.

    Purity matters for the last clause: adding the single invalid step p ▷ q to a complete calculus
for CPC leaves Cl(∅) unchanged, and for an impure τ even all of inst(τ ) need not trivialize
(Example 3.6). The proposition is the precise sense in which “the cautious assertion tier is
sound” is false without further structure. Caution is soundness relative to the realizable class
(Theorem 4.2), and systematic fallacies make the practice, not the target, the realizable object
(Remark 5.21).
    The obvious repair is to be cautious over hypotheses that may declare practice rules fallacious.
Suppose the practice is known, and let V = {Σ : Σ ⊆ ΣP }, where a candidate Σ reads “Σ is the
target and the rest of the practice is fallacious”. All candidates explain the positive data equally
well. Let Vd be the d-clean candidates and Vt′ those consistent with the refutations found by time
t.

Remark 7.7 (A Bayesian middle way). [T7 Remark 2.4 ] (proof sketch) Put a prior w on V and accept
s iff w{Σ ∈ Vt′ : s ∈
                    / RΣ } < w0 . By the argument of Theorem 4.14 this is sound at all times
                                (d)
for every target with w(Σ∗ ∪ Fres ) ≥ w0 , provided that set is clean, and it waits for the audit
automatically. It cannot break the symmetry of Theorem 7.21, where the rival diagnosis has the
same prior mass. We do not develop it; the audit-then-assert design below is simpler and has
explicit constants.

Reading. The dilemma is the formal shape of a familiar norm. A practice is presumed valid,
but the presumption is applied only after the hearing at which it could have been impeached. A
learner that presumes too early asserts the community’s fallacies; a learner that never presumes
asserts nothing, because refutational evidence cannot certify a rule. “Audit, then assert” is the
only stable design, and it is the design of TTL.

7.3    Blame: from bags to culprits
A contradiction from a designated context condemns a set of steps (Lemma 6.2). Which members
should a sound learner withhold?
Lemma 7.8 (Hitting-set duality). [T7 Lemma 2.3] Let U be finite, let K ⊆ 2U be upward closed with
∅∈/ K, and let C be its minimal members; call M ⊆ U clean if M ∈  / K. (a) The maximal clean sets
are exactly the complements of the minimal transversals (hitting sets) of C. (b) An element lies in
some minimal transversal iff it lies in some member of C. Hence {maximal clean sets} = U \ C,
                                                                 T                             S

and this set is clean. (c) The minimal transversal is unique iff every member of C is a singleton.
Lean: Blame.iInter_maxClean_eq


Proof of (a) and (b). (a) By upward closure, M is clean iff it contains no member of C, iff U \ M
meets every member of C. (b) If x ∈ C ∈ C, then (U \ C) ∪ {x} is a transversal (a member of C
inside C equals C), and any minimal transversal inside it must contain x to meet C. Conversely,
if x lies in a minimal transversal T , some E ∈ C has E ∩ T = {x}. The intersection of clean sets
is clean, since cleanness is closed downward. Part (c) is in Appendix F.3.

    This is Reiter’s theory of diagnosis from first principles (Reiter, 1987; de Kleer and
Williams, 1987), and (b) is the standard fact that the vertices of the transversal hyper-
graph are those of the minimal edges (Berge, 1989). The dictionary: components are prac-
tice schemas, “abnormal” means fallacious, conflicts are the negative bags of Section 6, and
diagnoses are hypotheses about which practice rules are fallacies. All three parts are for-
malized in Lean for a finite universe (Blame.isMaxClean_iff, Blame.iInter_maxClean_eq,
Blame.existsUnique_minTransversal_iff), and duality.py checks them on 4000 random
families with no failure. With U = ΣP and K = Conf d , every maximal candidate keeps ΣP \ Cd .
                                                                                         S

So Cd is exactly what a learner that presumes validity, and is cautious across the remaining
    S

ambiguity at the level of schemas, must withhold. At the level of instances it can do better

                                                64
(Proposition 7.6(c)), and that the withholding is forced for every sound learner is true at stable
depth (Theorem 7.21(b)) but can fail at finite depth (Theorem 7.21(c)).

Descent. Bags shrink to culprits when the argument can be evaluated line by line. Fix a
derivation π relative to [A : D] and let e+ π be the partial map on the judgments of π obtained
from e[A:D] by closing under two rules that use only the trusted steps of π: forward, if all premises
of a trusted step of π have value 1 then its conclusion gets 1; backward, if a trusted step of π has
conclusion value 0 and all but one premise at 1, then the remaining premise gets 0. A worklist
computes this fixed point with O(|π|φ) evaluations of e, where φ is the maximal fan-in. (Closing
under all trusted steps globally is not computable in general: in arithmetic, forward closure from
the true ∆0 sentences reaches exactly the true Σ1 sentences. It is also not needed.) To descend
on a refutation, start at its conclusion; at a node of value 0 produced by a step s, output s if all
premises of s have value 1, and otherwise move to a premise of value 0. The descent is unblocked
if at every visited node either all premises have value 1 or some premise has value 0.
Lemma 7.9 (Descent with trusted steps). [T7 Lemma 2.5, as repaired] Assume (WS) for [A : D],
with total valuation V . Then e+  π agrees with V wherever it is defined; in particular, a judgment
receiving both values certifies that [A : D] violates (WS). Let π be a d-refutation of B relative to
[A : D] whose descent is unblocked. Then: (a) the descent outputs an untrusted step s ∈ RB with
V -true premises and a V -false conclusion, so s ∈   / R∗ ; (b) computing e+π and descending costs
O(|π|φ) ≤ O(dφ) evaluations of e; (c) every σ ∈ B with s ∈ inst(σ) is a fallacy, so removing
all of them removes no genuine rule ( certified singleton blame); (d) for each such σ, {σ} is
a d-conflict: s together with the trusted steps of π that justify the values of its premises and
conclusion forms a d-refutation of {σ}.
Proof idea. Both propagation rules are V -sound because trusted steps preserve V -truth. The
descent follows nodes of value 0 strictly downward and leaves have value 1, so it stops at a step
with V -true premises and a V -false conclusion, which by (WS) is neither trusted nor in R∗ .
Part (d) extracts the refutation from the judgments of π; this is where size must count distinct
judgments (Definition 7.2(a)). Appendix F.5.
    This is Shapiro’s contradiction backtracing from algorithmic debugging and model inference
(Shapiro, 1981, 1983), the counterexample-to-blame step of Lemma 10.20, and Lakatos’s conversion
of a global counterexample into a local one (Lakatos, 1976). Two features are specific to this
setting. Trusted steps carry values backward through unevaluable nodes, which arithmetic needs
because ∀x θ is not ∆0 (Theorem 7.29(a)). And by (c) and (d), blame lands on schemas, with
no collateral, and every blamed schema is a singleton conflict. When the descent is blocked,
all we have is the bag {σ ∈ B : σ used in π}. The lemma itself is not formalized. The Lean
formalization of the related Lemma 10.20 covers descent for a total evaluation of a line-list
argument, including the final clause that the output step lies outside any truth-preserving
rule set (Blame.LineArg.descent, Blame.LineArg.descent_not_mem); the (WS) form with
trusted-step propagation, and part (d), are not formalized.

Reading: localization. In classical propositional logic and in arithmetic, world feedback
refutes nothing that coherence does not already refute: by Post-completeness an invalid schema
is incoherent in the empty context (Theorem 6.13), and a consistent extension of Q is ∆0 -sound
(Lemma 6.19). Its whole added value is that descent turns its refutations into singletons. World
feedback earns its keep by localization, not by refutation. Example 2.16 shows this in miniature:
the context A1 yields the bag {AC, MP}, and the valuation p = 0, q = 1 blames AC alone.

                                                 65
7.4    The learner TTL(d, δ)
Definition 7.10 (The two-tier learner). [T7 §3.1, as repaired] Inputs: the floor constants (∆, c, K, ᾱ);
an oracle Ref d (for A, W, Ttrust ) with either tie-breaking rule; a sandbox class HS of step sets
with prior w. The burn-in is
                  l 1       (1 + c)K m                                           l1        cK m
           N1 =        ln             ,      or, in noise-free mode,      N1 =        ln       ,
                   2∆2          δ                                                 λ         δ
with a known λ ≤ mini πi ρi in noise-free mode.
0. Phase 0 (t < N1 ). The assertion tier abstains. A query may be escalated to a human, or, if
   all its judgments lie in JW , answered by a separate world channel that accepts it iff it is not
   W -falsified; world-channel answers are not acceptances of the tier. Human steps are recorded;
   human answers to escalated queries are not added to the sample.
1. Practice identification (at t = N1 ). For each tag i with ni > ei samples, where ei =
   ⌊(ᾱi + ∆)N1 ⌋ (ei = 0 in noise-free mode), let σ̂i = mgu{lgg(Pi \ E) : |E| = ei }, the most
   general common instance of the trimmed lggs of the tag-i data Pi ; its instance set is the
   accepted set of the tag’s trimmed version space (Theorem 5.14). Tags with ni ≤ ei contribute
   nothing. Let P̂ = {σ̂i }.
2. Audit. Set B := P̂ and repeat: call r = Ref d (B); if r = NONE, return B; descend on r; if the
   descent outputs s, set B := B \ {σ ∈ B : s ∈ inst(σ)} and continue; if it is blocked, return
   B \ Cd (B) (the fallback).
       S

3. Freeze the returned set A. In Phase 1 (t ≥ N1 ) the tier accepts q iff q ∈ RA , decided by
   matching; other queries may be escalated.
4. Sandbox (all times). Oligarchic halving (Definition 6.4) over HS , for example over candidate
                                                                                         T
   generalizations of asserted schemas or candidate new rules: announce Bt = St with
   w(St ) ≥ 12 w(VSt ). A red team, which may be the prover or the learner’s own search, exhibits
   refutations π of Bt , and each deletes every h ⊇ Steps(π) \ Ttrust . Isolation: nothing from the
   sandbox enters RA as a primitive step. A conjecture κ is promoted only when a schematic
   RA -derivation of it is found, and then as a macro: a κ-instance is answered by expanding it
   into that derivation.
5. Truth maintenance. If the audit is re-run at a larger depth, every cached lemma or macro
   that uses a removed schema is invalidated.
     Two details were found by verification and simulation. In noise-free mode the budget must
be ei = 0: with ᾱi = 0 the noisy formula still gives ei = ⌊∆N1 ⌋ > 0, and then the event of
Theorem 5.7 does not imply identification. With one tag, one metavariable, two equiprobable
roots (λ = 12 ) and δ = 0.001, N1 = 16; identification fails with probability 0.077 for e = 4 and
3 · 10−5 for e = 0 (computed; noisefree.py). And with ni ≤ ei samples the trimmed version space
contains the empty rule, so its intersection is ∅, not “everything”; coding it as “accept all” made
TTL unsound in 12 of 20 simulated runs at N = 60.
Lemma 7.11 (Audit correctness). [T7 Lemma 3.1] Assume (WS) and P̂ = ΣP . (a) If no descent is
                                                            (d)
blocked, the audit returns a d-clean A with Σ∗ ⊆ A ⊆ Σ∗ ∪Fres , using at most |F |+1 calls to Ref d ;
every successful call removes at least one fallacy and no genuine rule. (b) If the fallback triggers
                                                                                                 (d)
at stage B0 , then Σ∗ ⊆ B0 , and A = B0 \ Cd (B0 ) is d-clean with Σ∗ \ Colld (B0 ) ⊆ A ⊆ Σ∗ ∪ Fres .
                                           S

If B0 = ΣP , then A = (Σ∗ \ Colld ) ∪ Fdsurv .

                                                   66
Proof. By Lemma 7.9(a),(c) each successful descent removes at least one schema, all of them
fallacies, so Σ∗ ⊆ B throughout, and there are at most |F | removals. (a) The returned B
is d-clean; for τ ∈ B \ Σ∗ , Σ∗ ∪ {τ } ⊆ B is clean because cleanness is closed downward, so
       (d)
τ ∈ Fres . (b) By Lemma 7.8 with U = B0 , A is the intersection of the maximal clean subsets of
B0 , hence clean. The left inclusion holds because Σ∗ ⊆ B0 and Colld (B0 ) = Σ∗ ∩ Cd (B0 ). If
                                                                                   S

τ ∈ A ∩ F were not residual, Σ∗ ∪ {τ } ⊆ B0 would contain a minimal conflict, which contains τ
by Lemma 7.4(a), contradicting τ ∈ A. The last claim is Proposition 7.6(c).

    Cost. The descent path costs at most |F | + 1 oracle calls and O((|F | + 1) d φ) evaluations
of e. The fallback needs Cd (B0 ): at most 2|B0 | oracle calls by brute force. From a cleanness
                          S

(membership) oracle, the minimal conflicts and the maximal clean sets of a monotone property
can be generated jointly in time quasi-polynomial in their total number (Bioch and Ibaraki, 1995;
Gurvich and Khachiyan, 1999), with the dualization algorithm of Fredman and Khachiyan (1996)
as the engine; the second family can be exponentially larger than the first, and both can be
exponential in |F |. At stable depth the fallback’s schema-level incompleteness is unavoidable
(Theorem 7.21(b)); its cost is the price of bags.

Mis-designated positions. Theorem 7.14 below assumes (WS) for every designated position.
When some positions may be wrong, as when an idealization is designated together with
background it contradicts, the audit can vote.

Proposition 7.12 (Voting audit). [T7 Prop 3.2, as repaired] Suppose at most m designated positions
violate (WS), it is not known which, and P̂ = ΣP . For each position p run the descent loop
of the audit relative to p alone, from Bp = ΣP and Blamedp = ∅, with the prefer-unblocked
oracle restricted to p. Stop when the oracle returns NONE (p is finished), when the descent
is blocked, or when ep is ill defined or e+
                                          π gives a judgment both values (then p is certified bad
and its blame is discarded); otherwise move every σ ∈ Bp with s ∈ inst(σ) into Blamedp . Let
votes(σ) = #{p : σ ∈ Blamedp }, counting blame from [∅ : ∅] as m + 1 votes if the world is known
to be truthful, and return A = ΣP \ {σ : votes(σ) ≥ m + 1}. Then (a) Σ∗ ⊆ A; (b) at most
|A|(|F | + 1) + m|Σ∗ | oracle calls are made; (c) call p complete if it is good and finished; then
A ∩ F ⊆ {τ ∈ F : #{p complete : Σ∗ ∪ {τ } is not d-clean relative to p} ≤ m}. For m = 0 and
all positions complete this is Lemma 7.11(a).

    Proof in Appendix F.6. Per-position loops matter: one smallest refutation per position can
hide a fallacy refutable at m + 1 positions behind another one. With m = 1, truthful positions
[q, p → q, ¬p : p, ¬q] and [¬r, r → t : ¬t] and practice {MP, AC, DA}, the per-position loops
remove denying the antecedent (refutable at both positions) and may keep AC (refutable at
one), while one-query voting kept both (computed; prop32_voting.py). The soft version of this is
Theorem 6.7.

against the coalition is at most log2 (1/w(h0 )), and with m false alarms and penalty factor β

                   + m ln β1 / ln 1+β

unanimity is decidable for finite St : s ∈ h∈St h iff s matches a schema of every h, and for

schema-valued hypotheses the intersection of instance sets is the instance set of the most general
unifier (Robinson, 1965; Plotkin, 1970; Reynolds, 1970).
Proof idea. (a) holds by construction. For (b), the proofs of Theorems 6.5 and 6.7 use only
that the protected hypothesis is never deleted, and a detection by π deletes only hypotheses
containing the untrusted steps of π, of which π is then a refutation. Appendix F.7, which also
shows what fails if promoted rules were primitive: a rule packaging the first half of a residual
fallacy’s refutation of size 2d yields one of size at most d.

Reading. How does cautious acceptance interact with the bold coalition? It does not. The
coalition is bold, and its errors are bounded by halving. The assertion tier is cautious, and its
soundness is certified by the audit. The only channel from the sandbox to the tier is derivation,
which cannot enlarge the closure (Lemma 3.1). Re-deriving sandbox lemmas inside RA is what
makes the separation safe; truth maintenance is the same point made across time. This resolves
the tension left by Sections 4 and 6: caution receives no signal from coherence (Proposition 6.3),
and boldness is unsound; so both tiers exist, and only the cautious one asserts.

7.5   The main theorem
Theorem 7.14 (Two-tier coherent inferential learning). [T7 Thm 4.1, as repaired] Assume (Floor)
(Assumption 7.1); realizability, i.e. the practice consists of tagged single schemas as in Defi-

exact for refutations of size at most d, with either tie-breaking rule. Run TTL(d, δ). There is an
event G with P(G) ≥ 1 − δ, depending only on the first N1 human steps, on which the following
hold for every adaptive prover and every red team.
  (i) Uniform soundness at all times. For t < N1 the tier accepts nothing. For t ≥ N1 every
      accepted step lies in R∗ ∪ RF (d) , so for every premise set B

    The non-monotonicity in (iv) is concrete (computed; depth_nonmono.py). Take the practice
{MP, AC}, no world, and the positions [A1 : ∅] and [r, s → r : s] with r, s formulas of size 7.
Then A = {MP, AC} for d < 9; A = ∅ for 9 ≤ d < 29, by fallback on the conflict {MP, AC}; and
A = {MP} for d ≥ 29, where the new conflict {AC} makes the old one non-minimal and restores
MP. Restored schemas are genuine or residual at the larger depth, so soundness is unaffected.

Corollary 7.15 (Truth-soundness). [T7 Cor 4.2, as repaired] Suppose R∗ is M-sound, every member
     (d)                         (d)
of Fres is M-valid (vacuous if Fres = ∅), and trusted steps, if used, are M-sound. Then on G
the reasoner derives only M-consequences, at all times and against all provers. This also holds
for a reasoner that chains world-channel steps with RA -steps, provided W is truth in M on JW .

   World-channel steps are truth-sound but need not be target-sound: for Σ∗ = {∧I, ∧E1 , ∧E2 }
the step ⊤ ▷ ⊤ ∨ ⊥ preserves truth but is not R∗ -derivable. That is why the world channel is
kept outside the tier, and why Theorem 7.14(i) concerns the tier only.

Remarks. (1) What is and is not certified. Genuine rules are never certified valid by anything in
the architecture: their assertion rests on positive data (anchoring with margin) plus presumption
after audit. Fallacies are certified invalid by descent, or implicated as a set by conflicts. Lemma 7.4
forces this asymmetry. (2) Realizability is load-bearing. If the human calculus has a rule outside
the class, identification fails, and nothing downstream repairs it; at best the audit removes an
over-general identified schema, which restores soundness but not completeness. Quantifier rules
and the induction schema are such rules (Corollary 7.28 and Theorem 7.29). If fallacies are cited
under genuine rules’ names, the per-tag class must be unions of schemas (Theorem 5.9). (3) Why
freeze. Freezing P̂ keeps the accounting to one event. Learning can continue at the cost of a
                                                   1
union bound over N ≥ N1 (an additive ≈ ln 2∆         2 inside the logarithm) and a re-audit whenever

P̂ changes. (4) Before N1 . Abstention can be softened by the world channel or by a trusted
base. Both are truth-sound (Corollary 7.15) but outside (i) unless they lie in Sound(R∗ ), and
Theorem 7.20 shows that no learner of the relevant kind can do much better on the schemas that
might be fallacies.

Reading. For the user’s question, the theorem says what “a learner of inference rules, condi-
tioned on coherence and the world” can deliver. It identifies the target calculus exactly, modulo
a residue that no refutational channel at depth d can see. It is sound at every moment against
any amount of search. Its costs are explicit: N1 human steps, at most |F | + 1 oracle calls on


                                                  69
the descent path, log2 (1/w(h0 )) detections in the sandbox. And it never asserts a rule because
the rule is coherent, only because the community uses it robustly and it survived the audit.
Coherence is a filter on imitation, not a source of rules.

7.6    Every ingredient is necessary
We now show that each ingredient of TTL is forced. Here a learner is any randomized procedure
that receives the same kinds of information as TTL, possibly with one channel removed: human
samples, the queries of a prover and red team, answers of Ref d and W , its own coins and,
optionally, escalation answers. Lower bounds use a fixed prover and red team, i.e. strategies
mapping the history and their own coins to queries, the same in every scenario. Escalation
answers, if used, follow the practice: they are drawn from a law depending only on the query and
on the practice (for instance 1[q ∈ RP ]), so they have the same law in scenarios with the same
practice. That covers Propositions 7.17 and 7.18 and Theorems 7.21 and 7.22; Theorem 7.20
compares different practices and needs an explicit assumption instead. A target-truthful labeller
(1[q ∈ R∗ ], the labelled setting of Section 4) is a different and stronger channel, and defeats
Theorems 7.20 and 7.21.
    Fix the practice with its frequencies, instance laws and noise law, and fix A, W, Ttrust . A target
Σ ⊆ ΣP is admissible if it satisfies (WS), equivalently is clean at every depth (Lemma 7.4(d)),
and each member of ΣP \ Σ has an instance outside Sound(RΣ ). All admissible targets share the
data law and the oracle answers (Lemma 7.4(c)) and satisfy (Floor) with the same constants.
  (d)
Fres (Σ) denotes the residue when Σ is the target.

Proposition 7.16 (Positive data are needed; trivial). [T7 Prop 5.1, as repaired] Let Σ1 ⊊ Σ2 be
calculi such that every subset of Σ2 is clean at every depth and RΣ2 ̸⊆ Sound(RΣ1 ). A learner
that receives only refutation evidence (oracle answers on schema sets of its choice, but no human
steps and no escalations) has the same view under the targets Σ1 and Σ2 . So it is unsound for
Σ1 or incomplete for Σ2 on the nonempty set RΣ2 \ Sound(RΣ1 ). Example: Σ1 = {∧I, ∧E1 , ∧E2 },
Σ2 = Σ1 ∪ {∨I1 } with classical evaluation. The non-derivability hypothesis is needed: for
Σ2 = Σ1 ∪ {A ∧ B ▷ B ∧ A}, accepting RΣ2 is both sound for Σ1 and complete for Σ2 .

    The proof is Lemma 7.4(c). This concerns soundness relative to the human calculus. In CPC,
structurality and closed evaluation decide schema validity (Lemma 7.24), so a learner wanting
only truth-soundness could enumerate schemas and assert the valid ones without human data;
positive data then only say which valid rules the practice uses. In arithmetic, positive data are
needed even for truth-completeness, because no refutational evidence ever certifies a true Π1
axiom.

     Σ∈T.

 (b) The following accept a falsified instance of some τ ∈ F \ Fres before the refutation implicating

Appendix F.9. Both qualifications were added in verification. The conditional probability given
h would be false for randomized learners: a learner that with probability ε accepts everything
from time 1 on, and otherwise runs TTL, is (δ + ε)-sound, yet given the history “q1 accepted
at time 1” it accepts every fallacy instance. And plain Sound(RΣ ) would make the intersection
trivial, since typically ∅ ∈ T , convicting TTL itself.
Proposition 7.18 (Negative evidence is needed). [T7 Prop 5.3] Let the practice be {∧I, AC} with
fixed frequencies. In world W1 the connectives have their classical tables, AC is a fallacy and
the target is {∧I}. In W2 the tables are classical except that → is read as ↔; then AC is valid
(from B and A ↔ B infer A) and the target is {∧I, AC}. A learner without refutation evidence
sees identically distributed data in both, so it is unsound under W1 or incomplete under W2 .
The closed instance (⊤, ⊥ → ⊤ ▷ ⊥) separates them: under W1 its premises are true and its
conclusion false, while under W2 the premise ⊥ ↔ ⊤ is false.
    This is Corollary 5.20 in two-world form.
Proposition 7.19 (Structurality is needed; trivial). [T7 Prop 5.4] (a) If hypotheses are instance
sets rather than schemas, each refutation removes finitely many instances, so a fallacy with
infinitely many invalid instances keeps some invalid instance asserted at every finite time by any
learner that asserts the practice minus the refuted instances. (b) Without uniformity coherence is
toothless (Proposition 6.37: Cn2 + {▷p17 }). (c) Without structure positive data cannot generalize:
for classes of finite step sets the cautious verifier accepts exactly the data (Section 5.4).
Theorem 7.20 (Burn-in is necessary: the rare refuter). [T7 Thm 5.5, as repaired in two rounds] Let
0 < π < 1, and let σ, τ, µ be schemas such that {σ, τ } and {σ, µ} are clean at every depth and
{µ, τ } is a d-conflict. Compare, with the same A, W, Ttrust and a fixed prover and red team:
scenario 1, practice {σ, τ, µ}, target {σ, µ}, F = {τ }, tag frequencies (1 − π)qσ , (1 − π)qτ , π; and
scenario 2, practice {σ, τ }, target {σ, τ }, F = ∅, frequencies qσ , qτ , where qσ + qτ = 1 and the
per-tag laws of σ and τ are the same in both. Assume
     (OE) at each round, every input to the learner other than the human sample (prover
     and red-team queries, answers of Ref d and W , escalation answers if any, the learner’s
     coins) is generated from the history by the same kernel in both scenarios.
Then some τ -instance lies outside Sound(R{σ,µ} ), and for every learner satisfying (OE), every
t and every such instance s: if in scenario 1 the learner accepts s at time t with probability at
most δ, then in scenario 2 it does so with probability at most δ(1 − π)−t . Hence, if δ + δ ′ < 1,
accepting s at time t in scenario 2 with probability at least 1 − δ ′ requires
